▶ position manager: structural and broker protection
  ✔ structural SL: a CONFIRMED close beyond the stop exits at that close (thesis invalidation), before the broker SL (1.1797ms)
  ✔ broker SL: an intrabar spike through 1.5 x structural + spread closes at exactly that distance plus slippage and is never widened (0.2824ms)
  ✔ thesis deterioration (3 adverse closes, beyond half R and the entry bar) exits early with a smaller loss than invalidation (0.2995ms)
  ✔ SELL mirror: spread is paid on the exit side and the structural stop is above the fill (0.2262ms)
✔ position manager: structural and broker protection (2.2567ms)
▶ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT
  ✔ profit milestone detection: PROFIT_AVAILABLE at the first close with MFE >= trigger R (0.75 R for D1, 1.0 R for D2) (0.4062ms)
  ✔ HOLD: with no profit available and no deterioration the manager holds and leaves protection unchanged (0.1588ms)
  ✔ HARVEST on weak continuation: a spike to the trigger followed by a weak close banks at that close (0.19ms)
  ✔ PROTECT + RUN on strong continuation: floor activates from the next bar, ratchets upward, and the floor touch banks the floor (0.2211ms)
  ✔ EXIT on adverse break during a protected run (harvest at the close, never below the floor) (0.1881ms)
  ✔ HARVEST when the remaining reward compresses (distance to TP2 < 0.5 ATR) (0.123ms)
  ✔ simple bank (CH-B) closes at the first close after 1 R (0.0837ms)
  ✔ production-like (CH-A) closes on retrace >= 0.5 of the best excursion after 1 R, or at the +30 USD monetary target (0.2998ms)
✔ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT (1.8904ms)

▶ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release
  ✔ acquires a free lock, writing a pid+timestamp lock file (5.5336ms)
  ✔ a second acquisition attempt fails while a live, fresh holder exists (simulated as a different pid) (3.6485ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock only removes a lock this SAME process holds (5.1081ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never removes another process's lock, even a stale one it did not itself reclaim (3.3681ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never throws even if the lock file is already gone (1.2683ms)
✔ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release (20.528ms)
▶ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim
  ✔ reclaims a lock whose holder process is no longer alive (4.7197ms)
  ✔ reclaims a lock older than maxAgeMs even if the holder PID still reports alive ("no permanent stale lock") (4.625ms)
  ✔ does NOT reclaim a fresh lock from a still-alive holder (3.3917ms)
  ✔ treats a corrupt/torn lock file as absent (fails open to acquisition, never crashes) (4.3657ms)
✔ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim (17.5781ms)
▶ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks
  ✔ tryAcquireCdpLock throws CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT if the CURRENT process already holds the lock (4.375ms)
  ✔ withCdpLock rejects with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT (not a hang) if fn() tries to re-acquire the SAME lock nested inside itself (4.5341ms)
✔ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks (9.1675ms)
▶ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout
  ✔ polls until a busy lock frees up, then acquires it (proves serialization: a second "operation" WAITS its turn rather than racing) (51.9782ms)
  ✔ throws CDP_LOCK_TIMEOUT rather than hanging forever if the lock never frees up (41.9923ms)
✔ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout (94.5958ms)
▶ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases
  ✔ releases after fn() resolves successfully (4.45ms)
  ✔ releases after fn() throws (a WAIT-shaped/error result never leaves the lock held) (4.7572ms)
  ✔ two sequential withCdpLock() calls on the SAME path never see each other's lock (serialized, not deadlocked) (7.9226ms)
  ✔ proves cross-process mutual exclusion via a simulated different-pid holder: a competing acquisition never wins while the (other-process) holder is live and fresh, and wins immediately once it is not (15.328ms)
  ✔ within the SAME process, a second (non-nested but concurrent) withCdpLock() call on the SAME path fails fast with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT rather than hanging or double-entering fn() (29.2002ms)
  ✔ leaves no leftover .tmp- files behind after normal sequential use (Windows-race hardening cleans up its own losing temp files) (47.1137ms)
✔ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases (109.6136ms)

▶ setup / trigger rules on synthetic bars (information-time)

  ✔ overextension_state=ENTRY_LATE with an otherwise-complete BUY is a contradiction (0.1587ms)
  ✔ BUY and SELL cannot both appear in a single fixture (ACTION is a single field) — structurally impossible by the contract's own shape (0.2032ms)

▶ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array
  ✔ every "for i = 0 to array.size(IDENT) - 1" is immediately preceded (skipping blank/comment lines) by "if array.size(IDENT) > 0" (2.5355ms)
  ✔ the P5 OUTCOME RESOLUTION backward loop (for safe in-loop array.remove) is guarded against an empty openSignals array exactly like every forward loop (0.508ms)
  ✔ array.remove() now legitimately exists (P5 review/fix), but ONLY as the one safe in-loop removal inside the guarded, backward-iterating P5 resolution loop — no other unsafe indexed-collection function (array.first/array.last/array.pop/array.insert) exists anywhere (1.0067ms)
  ✔ array.shift() call sites remain safe: P2's two capacity-eviction sites remain guarded by "size > maxActiveLevels" preceded by array.push() (guaranteeing size >= 1); P5's recentHistory (closed-only) eviction is guarded by "size >= p5MaxTrackedSignals" instead (a capacity check ahead of the push, not a post-push overflow check — still provably safe, since array.shift() on an empty array is never reachable there either: the guard only ever removes an EXISTING oldest element to make room, and array.push() unconditionally follows it in the same block). Critically, openSignals (the never-evicted OPEN tracker) must have NO array.shift() call anywhere. (2.1243ms)
  ✔ the debug-table convenience reads (last-level consumed/swept) remain guarded by their own ternary size>0 check — unchanged by this patch (0.6796ms)
